What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: What scared me enough to come in is… at work, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I’ve been hearing voices through the monitor speakers. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nBut the thing is, the channels are muted. And no one else hears it. It’s been really frightening.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like commands, no. I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, even when the channels are muted. I’ve had times where I tried to tell myself things would be okay, but not where I felt unusually confident or optimistic.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't need much sleep and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. If I don’t sleep much, I usually feel worn down, not rested or energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had a time like that. I get frustrated sometimes, but not extremely irritable or like it took over for days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can’t think of a time where I felt on top of the world or unusually happy like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the beliefs about being careless or dangerous, as the voices suggest, are true even when others tell you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes… a lot of the time I do. Even if someone tells me I’m not careless, I keep going back over every camera angle I missed or any call I made late, and it feels like proof that I’m dangerous at my job. Part of me really believes that if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood made it hard for you to do your daily activities or work?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s made work harder. I’ve already made mistakes on shift paperwork, and I’ve had to kind of pull back from activities and seeing friends because I just can’t keep up like I used to.\n\nAt home I’m mostly just doing the basics. I’m not really cooking much, or having people over, or getting to projects I used to take care of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually confident or optimistic?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’ve had times where I tried to tell myself things would be okay, but not where I felt unusually confident or optimistic.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't need much sleep and still felt rested?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. If I don’t sleep much, I usually feel worn down, not rested or energized.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had a time like that. If I’m not sleeping, I don’t feel energetic—I just feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your low mood significantly improved, even if only for a little while?\n\nPatient: Not really. Most days it’s like this heavy gray feeling is there before I even get out of bed. I can’t remember a time where I felt unusually high or full of energy like that.
